# Protests over Mykhailo Fedorov leaving the Defence Ministry — media collection

**Snapshot: 6 August 2026.** The protest is in its **twenty-second consecutive day (16 July – 6 August) and still ongoing**, so **figures remain preliminary**. Each city carries the **peak over the 22-day wave**, not the count at any single hour, and now also carries how long it lasted: `first_day`, `last_day`, `days_active` and `peak_day`. Where a crowd was still growing when last recorded, its figure is a lower bound; every figure is therefore given with the time it is valid for. The days 5–22 rows added in this run are tagged `run = 6 Aug`; earlier rows keep their original run tags (`13:00`, `15:00`, `18 Jul`, `19 Jul`, `19 Jul PM`) unchanged.

> The tables below are **generated** from `cities.csv` and `publications.csv` by `build_report.py`. Edit the CSVs, not this file. `check_consistency.py` verifies that the artifacts agree.

> **Quotes stay in the original Ukrainian.** `quote_uk` is defined as the outlet's exact words; translating a quote turns evidence into paraphrase, which is the failure mode documented in warning 3. English glosses live in `quote_en`.

## CONTEXT

**⚠️ Updated on 6 August (days 5–22 added).** The previous snapshot closed on 19 July with the wave in its fourth day and the note that "further days are likely". They were: the protests have not stopped since, and this run extends the dataset by eighteen days. The 15:00 snapshot on 16 July recorded that no candidacy for defence minister had been submitted and the ministry sat empty; it also carried an earlier error, since corrected, that the president had already submitted Ihor Klymenko's candidacy. Both were overtaken by events: on 17 July the Cabinet named an **acting** minister — not Klymenko but Yevhen Khmara.

What happened:

- **14.07.2026** — parliament accepted the resignation of Yuliia Svyrydenko's government. The entire cabinet, Fedorov included, became acting ministers.
- **15.07.2026** — Fedorov confirmed he was leaving the post, which he had held since 14.01.2026.
- **16.07.2026, from 09:00** — «cardboard protests» in 33 cities. The Kyiv action was called by veteran **Dmytro Koziatynskyi**, one of the organisers of the July 2025 protests in defence of NABU and SAPO.
- **16.07.2026, 12:12** — parliament elected **Serhii Koretskyi** prime minister, «За — **289**» (Yaroslav Zhelezniak; confirmed by LB at 12:13). The Defence and Foreign ministries were **deliberately excluded** from the cabinet vote.
- **17.07.2026** — protests ran for a **second day**. In Kyiv the evening crowd on Ivan Franko Square reached **«близько 3 тисяч людей»** (LB, own correspondent) and, by several accounts, **exceeded the first day** (Kyiv24: «зібрала значно більше людей, ніж у перший день акції»). Demands widened: besides Fedorov's return, protesters now called for the **dismissal of Commander-in-Chief Oleksandr Syrskyi** and the **recall of MPs** from a month-long recess. Zelensky publicly confirmed for the first time a **conflict between Syrskyi and Fedorov**, calling it a problem of national scale.
- **17.07.2026, 17:39** — the Cabinet, in agreement with the President, named **Yevhen Khmara acting Minister of Defence** and Andrii Sybiha acting Foreign Minister (LB). Fedorov had **declined** Zelensky's offer of an adviser post.
- **18.07.2026** — protests ran for a **third day**. The Kyiv action on Ivan Franko Square ran from ~15:00 and reached **«близько 2 тисяч людей»** by 21:45 (Suspilne, now reporting Kyiv directly), below the day-2 peak. **Mykolaiv grew to «понад 150 людей»** (from 45 on day 1, crossing into 100–999) and Kharkiv police counted **«близько 400»** at 20:00 — both wave highs for their cities. Solidarity rallies were also held **abroad** — Warsaw, Brussels, Sydney — outside the map's scope. Late on 18 July Zelensky held long talks with **Fedorov and Syrskyi** and announced a «рішення щодо армії» (a decision on the army) without reversing the reshuffle; Yevhen Khmara (acting SBU head) continues as acting Defence Minister. A Verkhovna Rada petition to keep Fedorov (author Svitlana Sirko, opened 16 July) is gathering signatures.
- **19.07.2026** — protests ran for a **fourth day** and drew the **largest crowd yet in Kyiv: «понад 5000 людей»** on Ivan Franko Square (Interfax-Ukraine via LIGA), above the day-2 peak. Actions continued in ~15 cities — Lviv, Dnipro, Kharkiv («понад 400»), Odesa, Mykolaiv, Ivano-Frankivsk («приблизно 250») and others — and reached **two cities new to the map, Mukachevo and Kamianske**. The Verkhovna Rada **went into recess until 18 August without appointing a defence minister** — Klymenko's candidacy was never submitted (Ivan Vyhivsky took the interior ministry) and **Yevhen Khmara remains acting**. The demand — Fedorov back, Syrskyi out — is unmet, so the protests continue.

- **20–23.07.2026** — the wave settled into a **daily evening routine** and shrank sharply from its opening days. Kyiv fell to «сотні киян» on 20 July and to **«близько 500 людей»** on 22 and 23 July, a figure four separate newsrooms carried identically (Suspilne, Ukrainska Pravda, Hromadske, Korrespondent). Three towns entered the record here, all of them missed by the first snapshot: **Izmail** (Suspilne Odesa states its rallies had run «з 16 липня», so from day 1), **Kalush** («понад 50 людей другий день поспіль», Suspilne Ivano-Frankivsk) and **Sheptytskyi** (the local outlet Raion.Sheptytskyi, third day running). On 23 July protesters added a demand against the appointment of **Vitalii Kim** to the Ministry of Veterans.
- **24.07.2026** — the deadline the protesters had set Zelensky for personnel decisions passed unmet, and organiser Koziatynskyi declared an **open-ended protest** («безстрокові протести») on Ivan Franko Square. Kyiv «близько 500 людей».
- **26–30.07.2026** — the **trough of the wave**. DW headlined «"Картонкові протести" в Києві: учасників стає дедалі менше»; NV ran an explainer on why the actions were fading. Kyiv fell to «близько 150 осіб» on 26 July; Mykolaiv's dialogue-police counts slid from 55 to 25; on 28 July Koziatynskyi called off the Kyiv action because of a missile threat and announced a march instead of the daily rally. **Syrskyi was removed** in this window — by 28 July Suspilne Kharkiv was quoting protesters as «Сирського прибрали, то чому Федоров досі не на своєму місці» — and **Drapatyi** was appointed.
- **31.07.2026** — the **peak of the entire wave**. The «Єдина протестна хода» marched from Shevchenko Park to Ivan Franko Square; Ukrinform and Fakty ICTV put Kyiv at **«близько 6 тисяч осіб»**, which Bukvy attributes to the **police** — the only official crowd estimate the wave ever produced, in any city, on any day. Koziatynskyi called it the most massive protest since the Revolution of Dignity and said roughly **30 cities** marched or rallied the same day; Censor.NET rendered it as the largest in twelve years, Kyiv Post as «десятки тисяч». **Zelensky removed Fedorov from the National Security and Defence Council that same day.** Lviv, Dnipro, Odesa, Chernivtsi, Rivne, Ternopil, Poltava, Kharkiv, Uzhhorod and Vinnytsia all held their own marches.
- **01–06.08.2026** — after the march the numbers collapsed and did not recover: Kyiv «понад 100 осіб» on 1 August, «близько 200 мітингувальників» on 3 August (DW), «близько ста двадцяти людей» on 4 August. Mykolaiv reached **twelve people** on 3 August, Uzhhorod twelve on 5 August. Dnipro changed format to a **silent protest** («тиха акція», «без кричалок») and counted its 23rd local action by 6 August. **Umerov and Klymenko were given new posts on 3 August**; the Office of the President was still answering questions on 5 August about whether Fedorov would return. On **6 August the protests entered their 22nd consecutive day.**

**The finding of this run: the wave did not fade, it spiked and then thinned — and the reshuffle still stands.** Fedorov has not been reappointed and was removed from the NSDC on the day of the largest protest; Syrskyi, whose dismissal the protesters demanded from day 2, **was** removed, and Drapatyi appointed. The movement's peak came on **day 16, not day 1** — the reverse of the usual decay — and its participants have now stood for **22 consecutive days**, in some cities every single one of them. The map records **where** and **how large**, over 16 July – 6 August; it does not adjudicate the political outcome.

## ⚠️ METHODOLOGICAL WARNINGS

**1. Suspilne's national round-up is a stale mirror of its own regional desks.** The text matches word for word; only the figure differs. As of 15:00 the round-up (updated 11:41) still showed Mykolaiv «26 людей» (regional desk: «45», 11:02) and Dnipro «близько 100» (regional desk: «близько 300», 15:00). **No figure from the round-up can be used without checking the regional desk.**

**2. Kharkiv inverts that pattern.** There the round-up gives more («щонайменше 300», unattributed) than the regional desk («близько 200», police spokesperson, 12:10). The direction is opposite to Mykolaiv and Dnipro, so "the regional desk is always higher" fails as a rule. Time resolves it: MediaPort on the scene watched the crowd grow from «близько 100» to «орієнтовно до 300» within half an hour, matching «щонайменше 300». The police figure of «близько 200» at 12:10 is a later snapshot, after part of the crowd had left. **The daily peak is about 300.**

**3. Search snippets fabricate figures.** Phrases recorded that **appear in no page body**: «кілька тисяч» (Kyiv), «близько тисячі» (Kharkiv), «близько 400 молодих людей» (Lviv). Snippets also credited an AFP quote to Reuters, and a Kyiv Post quote to AP. **Every figure here comes from the body of a page that was actually retrieved.**

**4. Headlines inflate.** TSN: headline «вийшли **тисячі людей**», body text «сотні» at most. Gal-Info: «**масштабна** акція», no figure anywhere in the body.

**5. Most "contradictions" are crowd growth, not conflicting sources.** Dnipro 100→300, Cherkasy 30→100, Khmelnytskyi 50→100, Kharkiv 100→300 within half an hour. Every figure is therefore given **with its time**.

**6. `dateModified` is not evidence of an update.** Ternopil, Zaporizhzhia and Chernivtsi carry fresh modification stamps (15:07–15:30) over text that is word-for-word the morning version: the CMS touched the page without a recount. Trust only markers inside the text, such as «ОНОВЛЕНО. 13:30».

**7. Telegram serves timestamps in UTC.** Kyiv is UTC+3. Zhelezniak's posts need +3 (anchored: «289» at 09:12 UTC = 12:13 Kyiv in LB). Carrying his timestamps over as Kyiv time shifts the chronology by three hours.

**8. Ukrainian inflects city names, so a nominative-case grep returns a false zero.** Searching «Кременчук» in KP.UA's text returns nothing, because the text reads «Кременчу**ці**». Same class of trap as encoding: **dumskaya.net and monitor.cn.ua serve windows-1251**, and reading them as UTF-8 also returns a false zero.

**9. An error in a source, left uncorrected:** Censor.NET's Mykolaiv section says Fedorov was dismissed «з посади **міністра цифрової трансформації**». This contradicts the rest of its own text and every other source. Recorded as published.

**10. The figure selection rule.** Take the figure that agrees across several independent sources, is the latest in time and the largest: the **daily peak**, not the count at a given hour. The aim is to estimate how many people turned out at maximum over the whole day. This follows from warning 5: crowds grew, so an early figure is not a smaller estimate but a different moment.

**11. The 19 July run (day 3) refined the «10 тис.» figure and reran discovery.** The day-2 evening «понад 10 тис.» in Kyiv is **not** snippet fabrication after all: Interfax-Ukraine reported it as a wire story (17 Jul, 18:28; retrieved here via Espreso's citation «за даними „Інтерфакс-Україна"»). It is still not used as the peak — LB's own correspondent on the scene gave «близько 3 тисяч», a ~3× gap that is flagged, not silently resolved — but it is a reported figure, not the fabrication class of day 1's «кілька тисяч». Aggregators (Telegraf, mignews) also circulated it. Separately, the v3 discovery method was rerun across the whole wave (gazetteer-grep over every downloaded round-up, batch tier-A queries, targeted city searches) and surfaced **no verifiable city beyond the 25**: the four cities a competitor's Telegram map flagged (Irpin, Boryspil, Kalush, Drohobych) appear in no round-up body and no city-specific report, and every gazetteer hit was a false positive — the organiser's surname «Козятинський», the President's name «Володимир», war-attack city lists (Ізюм, Марганець), a media brand («Еспресо.Біла Церква», which the outlet separately reported as *not* protesting), the ammunition-depot case «Вишневе». **A date trap persists:** several pieces search surfaces as «second/third day» are in fact dated a day earlier — read the article's own date before trusting a day label.

**12. The 6 August run (days 5–22): three traps specific to collecting a long wave.** *(a) Articles cite other days' figures.* Assigning every number in a page to the page's own date produced a Kyiv «20 000» on 3 August — a retrospective mention of the 31 July march inside a 3 August report — and a «10 000» on 22 July, which was LIGA recapping 17–18 July. Figures are therefore only accepted when the sentence carries no conflicting date and no retrospective marker («нагадаємо», «раніше», «напередодні», «за час протестів»). *(b) Regional-desk page furniture manufactures protests.* Suspilne's regional pages wrap every article in "related articles" teasers, so a Dnipro report carries the string «Суспільне Івано-Франківськ», and a Kolomyia page carries «До 785-річчя Коломиї» — which parsed as a crowd of 785. Two filters were needed: any sentence repeated across six or more documents is page furniture and is dropped, and the article's own headline must be about the protests. *(c) Numbers that are not crowds.* «розпочалася близько 9:00» became nine people, the photo-gallery counter «1 / 14» became fourteen, and «понад 17 років живе у Дніпрі» became seventeen. *A fourth trap survived into the data and was caught only at verification:* stripping photo credits before storing a quote left 19 quotes mutilated rather than verbatim; they were re-extracted from the unmodified body, and the five that could not be recovered cleanly were demoted to «невідомо» rather than published as approximations.

**13. Day numbering in the sources is not reliable; the calendar date is.** On 28 July UKR.NET called it day 13 while ANTIKOR called it day 11. Dnipro.media numbers its own local actions and reached the 23rd by 6 August, one more than the wave has days, because some days carried two actions. Every row here is anchored to the calendar date of the event, never to an outlet's day label.

**14. Headline inflation, caught again — this time in a wire agency.** Ukrinform's day-3 Uzhhorod piece is headlined «кількість мітингувальників... **зросла до двох сотень**», but the body contains no absolute number at all, only its own correspondent's «нині на зібрання прийшло майже вдвічі більше людей, аніж раніше». Under warning 4 the body governs, so Uzhhorod stays `<100` on its verified counts (police «близько 50» on day 1, «близько 20» on 30 July, «лише 12 людей» on 5 August) and is marked contested.

> **Verdict:** Peak on day 4 (19 Jul) of an ongoing wave. Day 2 (17 Jul), evening, Ivan Franko Square: «близько 3 тисяч людей» (LB, own correspondent), which several newsrooms say exceeded day 1 — Kyiv24: the square «зібрала значно більше людей, ніж у перший день акції», the largest influx around 20:00; solidarity signs also appeared at Atlas Festival. Day-2 morning was «близько 200 учасників» (Suspilne, NV). Day-1 peak was about two thousand (Interfax «близько двох тисяч», LB «понад тисяча», Kyiv Post «several thousand», AFP «more than a thousand»); Suspilne's «сотні» was a stale round-up line, not its own Kyiv reporting. «понад 10 тис.» for the day-2 evening was a real Interfax-Ukraine report (per Espreso citing «Інтерфакс-Україна»), not the snippet-fabrication class of day 1's «кілька тисяч»; still, LB's own correspondent on the scene gave «близько 3 тисяч», so that conservative figure is kept as the peak and the ~3x discrepancy flagged (aggregators Telegraf and mignews circulated «10 тис.» too). Suspilne still has no Kyiv desk, but a dedicated Kyiv article now exists (LB), unlike day 1. No police or city-administration estimate. Day 3 (18 Jul) went ahead on the same square (called by Dmytro Koziatynskyi): the action ran from ~15:00, and Suspilne — covering Kyiv directly this time, unlike day 1 — recorded «близько 300» at 19:30 rising to «близько 2 тисяч» at 21:45, below the day-2 peak, so the two-day figure stands. Demands widened to Syrskyi's dismissal and Fedorov's reappointment; solidarity rallies were also held abroad (Warsaw, Brussels, Sydney). Day 4 (19 Jul) is the largest yet: Interfax-Ukraine (via LIGA) reported «понад 5000» on Ivan Franko Square, now the wave peak — the late-evening «кількасот» (5.ua, 21:31) is a thinned slice. The ministry is still unfilled (Khmara acting, Klymenko's candidacy not submitted, Rada in recess to 18 Aug), so the protests continue into a fourth day. DAY 16 (31 Jul) IS THE NEW WAVE PEAK and the first Kyiv figure of the whole wave with an official source. The «Єдина протестна хода» ran from Shevchenko Park to Ivan Franko Square; Ukrinform put it at «близько 6 тисяч осіб» and Fakty ICTV at «близько 6 тис.», and Bukvy attributes that number to the police via Radio Svoboda and Ukrinform — so under §3.4 it outranks every editorial estimate and is taken as the peak. It is nonetheless marked contested, because the published range is extraordinarily wide: «близько тисячі» (Kyiv24 via Focus and Tvoe Misto, 19:54, before the march filled), «кілька тисяч» (Radio Svoboda own correspondents), «близько 15 тисяч» / «до 15 000 осіб» — organisers' own count (shelter.in.ua, UKR.NET), and «десятки тисяч» (Kyiv Post). Organiser Dmytro Koziatynskyi called it the most massive protest since the Revolution of Dignity and said roughly 30 cities held a march or rally the same day; Censor.NET rendered this as the largest in 12 years. Zelensky removed Fedorov from the National Security and Defence Council the same day. The wave then continued at a much lower level: «близько 200 мітингувальників» on 3 Aug (DW) and «близько ста двадцяти людей» on 4 Aug (Novyny.LIVE). Days 5-15 ran between «сотні» and «близько 500» (Suspilne, UP, LB, Hromadske, Censor agreeing on «близько 500» on 22 and 24 July). Protests were declared open-ended on 24 July.

> **Verdict:** Two waves. The morning action ran until 11:00, then participants dispersed and regathered at 13:00 ahead of the parliamentary session. The «ОНОВЛЕНО. 13:30» block reads «понад 60, і люди продовжують підходити»; below it, «понад сто людей» about the 13:00 gathering. Day 2 (17 Jul): a second-day action was called for 18:00 opposite the City Garden (OBOZ); Day 4 (19 Jul) was far larger: «На вулиці Дерибасівській зібралися близько тисячі людей» (24 Kanal, citing Suspilne) — a ~7× surge from day 1 and the Odesa wave peak. «Близько тисячі» sits at the 1000 boundary and is single-source, so it is marked contested and taken as 1000+ by the «близько сотні»→100–999 convention; day-1 «понад сто» and the day-2 18:00 action are the earlier points. Day 16 (31 Jul): «близько 200 осіб» took part in the march (Suspilne Odesa), an order below the contested day-4 «близько тисячі», which remains the peak.

IMI later analysed how Chernihiv media covered the wave, noting Suspilne Chernihiv filed more than ten pieces, the most of any local outlet.

> **Verdict:** The only city where Suspilne explicitly records growth as of 15:00. Trajectory: «близько ста» (09:34), «кількасот» (10:53), «близько 300». The article dateModified reads 15:00, but the text matches the 12:23 version: a last revision is not a recount. Day 2 (17 Jul): the action continued, again «близько 300 людей» (OBOZ, likely carrying the Suspilne Dnipro figure); the peak is unchanged. Settled to took place. The most persistent location after Kyiv: Dnipro.media numbered its own local actions and reached the 23rd by 6 August, more than the wave's day count, because some days carried two. The 31 July march drew «кілька сотень» (Bukvy) / «близько ста» (Suspilne, Censor, OBOZ); by 5-6 August the format changed to a silent protest, «тиха акція» with «щонайменше 50 людей».

> **Verdict:** «Акція не має терміну тривалості». Correspondents give «орієнтовно дві сотні людей», police «орієнтовно 200». The early 0342.ua slice, «десятки», is 09:34, while the crowd was gathering. Day 2 (17 Jul): people gathered again on the square by the oblast administration (Suspilne); no separate day-2 count was confirmed, so «орієнтовно 200» stands. (A day-1 relay, Halyckyj korespondent via Censor, gave «близько трьох сотень».) Settled to took place. Among the most persistent locations, covered daily by Suspilne Ivano-Frankivsk, pravda.if.ua, Informator and Firtka. The band over days 11-17 was 50-100, rising to 200 for the 1 August march («У Франківську на 17 день акцій до протестної ходи долучилися 200 людей», Suspilne).

> **Verdict:** Peak of roughly 300, corroborated by two independent sources: MediaPort on the scene, which watched the crowd grow from 100 to 300 within half an hour, and the Suspilne national round-up («щонайменше 300»). Police via Suspilne Kharkiv gave «близько 200» at 12:10, a later snapshot after part of the crowd had left, not a refutation of the peak. «Десятки» at 09:24 is a slice taken while the crowd was still gathering. One action, not two: Svobody Square, Shevchenko Garden and Universytet metro are one site; during an air-raid alert the rally moved into the metro underpass for about ten minutes. Day 3 (18 Jul): police counted «близько 400 людей» at 20:00 (Suspilne day-3 round-up), the wave high; the category is unchanged. Police counted the city repeatedly through the second week: «близько 350 людей» at 20:00 on 20 July, «близько 250» on 24 July, «до 150» on 25 July, «близько 180» on 26 July, «близько 90» on 28 July, «близько півсотні» on 30 July. The day-4 450 remains the peak.

> **Verdict:** A rare case: an own count with two time points. Censor independently confirms «близько 60 учасників» at 09:30. The city was absent from the announcement. REVISED UPWARD in the 6 Aug run: on day 4 co-organiser Denys Markov told Suspilne «до акції долучилися півтори сотні людей», moving the city from <100 to 100–999. Police counted 66 on 20 July, and Hrechka reported «близько 30 учасників» as the stable core by 26 July.

> **Verdict:** Two waves. «Акцію призупинили через півтори години»; participants regathered at 13:30 and the action continued as of 14:00. Suspilne gives no figure for the second wave. Lutsk added a demand absent elsewhere: the dismissal of Commander-in-Chief Oleksandr Syrskyi. The Suspilne article dateModified moved from 14:04 to 15:57 during verification while the body stayed byte-identical. Day 2 (17 Jul): «орієнтовно пів сотні людей» on Teatralnyi Maidan in the morning (Suspilne, NV); the day-1 «близько сотні» remains the peak. Settled to took place.

> **Verdict:** Suspilne Lviv has an article but gives no figure in the body, hence ZAXID as the source. Svobody Avenue, by the Shevchenko monument. Days 12-21 sit in a narrow band: «близько сотні» (27 Jul, NV) against «близько 150» from the police the same day (UP), «близько 200» on 28 and 30 July, «кілька сотень» for the 31 July march to Rynok Square, «близько півтора сотні» on 5 August.

> **Verdict:** Three-day peak; the crowd grew across the wave. Day 1 (16 Jul): «Біля міської ради зібралися 45 людей» (Suspilne Mykolaiv, 11:02; a march along Soborna Street; the national round-up carried the stale «26 людей»), <100. Day 3 (18 Jul): «понад 150 людей» joined (Suspilne day-3 round-up), moving Mykolaiv into 100–999. The clearest decay curve in the dataset, counted almost daily by the dialogue police via Suspilne Mykolaiv: 80 (20 Jul), 35 (21 Jul), 30 (22 Jul), «понад 50» (24 Jul), 55 (25 Jul), 32 (26 Jul), 35 (27 Jul), 25 (28 Jul), and «близько 12 людей» on 3 August. Hard.City noted people gather daily at 18:00 and stand until at least 20:00.

> **Verdict:** At 13:00 Suspilne carried no figure, and NV's «близько сотні» was treated as a false attribution. NV was in fact ahead of Suspilne, which now carries that figure. Local outlets poltava.to and kolo.news counted «близько 70» themselves. Theatre Square. REVISED in the 6 Aug run: the peak is day 5, not day 1 — «близько 20:00 у центрі міста зібралися орієнтовно 130 людей» (Suspilne Poltava). «Майже вісім десятків» on 24 July, «понад сотню» for the 31 July march.

> **Verdict:** The only city where the action was interrupted. At 10:00 protesters stopped and went to Independence Square to pay respects to the family of a fallen defender. REVISED UPWARD in the 6 Aug run: Rivne peaked well above its day-1 fifty. «Майже 100 людей» on 21 July (Suspilne), «понад 50» on 24 July, «понад 40» on 27 July, «приблизно тридцять» on 28 July, then back to «приблизно сто людей» for the 31 July march and «близько ста людей» on 1 August (Rayon.in.ua). Chetverta Vlada counted «близько 150 людей» itself on 24 July, above Suspilne's figure for the same day.

> **Verdict:** NEW in the 6 Aug run, and a direct correction to the previous snapshot's §5. Kalush was one of the four towns a rival Telegram map flagged on 17 July and for which the 19 July run found NO verifiable media source, so it was left off the map. It now has one: Suspilne Ivano-Frankivsk's own correspondent reported «понад 50 людей другий день поспіль» on 20 July — «другий день» dating the first Kalush action to 19 July. Ivano-Frankivsk oblast, ~65k.

> **Verdict:** The 25th location and the smallest action. Found only by the gazetteer sweep of 499 cities; it appears in no round-up. Single source; a targeted search produced no second confirmation. Outside the town hall. Far more persistent than the day-1 snapshot suggested: Suspilne Ivano-Frankivsk's daily round-ups place Kolomyia among the protesting towns on most days of the wave, and UKR.NET reported on 26 July that «В Коломиї активісти не припиняють акції». No figure after day 1.

> **Verdict:** A different modality, owing to proximity to the front: single pickets by the Sadko fountain plus cardboard signs left on railings and benches around the city. KP.UA reported the action had been cancelled. Refuted by an on-scene report with photographs: «Попри безпекову ситуацію, у Сумах люди вийшли на вулиці». The signs are being torn down. Suspilne has no Sumy material at all. Day 2 (17 Jul): «близько двох десятків людей» again (OBOZ). Settled to took place. No coverage after day 2 in any source checked in the 6 Aug run.

> **Verdict:** The 15:00 run added police attribution; at 13:00 the figure had no source. Still a single-source location: no Zakarpattia outlet covered the topic. Participants opened with a minute of silence. Uzhhorod was absent from the announcement. CONTESTED after the 6 Aug run, and a textbook headline-inflation case. Ukrinform's day-3 piece is headlined «В Ужгороді кількість мітингувальників на підтримку Федорова зросла до двох сотень», but its body gives no absolute number at all — only its own correspondent's «нині на зібрання прийшло майже вдвічі більше людей, аніж раніше». Under §3.6 the figure is taken from the body, so the verified absolute counts stand: «близько 50» (police, day 1), «близько 20 осіб» on 30 July (Censor, OBOZ), and «лише 12 людей» on 5 August, where participants told Novyny.LIVE that «зазвичай протестувальників понад три десятки». Uzhhorod is among the most persistent locations in the dataset.

Neither publishes a crowd figure and no piece by ZHAR.INFO itself was retrievable, so the town is carried as a relay-sourced location. Khmelnytskyi oblast, ~100k.

Found only through UKR.NET relays, whose originals resolve to the local outlet Raion.Sheptytskyi; that outlet is not indexed by the search route used here, so the town appears in no national round-up. Its second-day piece (19 July) and third-day piece (21 July) date the first action to 18 July. The town council's position on the new rules for mass events is discussed in the third-day piece. No crowd figure. Lviv oblast, formerly Chervonohrad, ~65k.

## Table 2 — summary by city

Each figure is the **peak over the 22-day wave (16 July – 6 August)**: agreed across several independent sources, latest, largest. `contested` marks a genuine disagreement; both versions are kept in the data. **Days** counts the distinct days on which a protest in that city is documented by at least one publication in this dataset — it is a floor on how long the city protested, not a claim that it did not protest on the other days.

### Second day (17 July), added in the 18 July run

Street actions recurred in at least Kyiv, Lutsk, Mykolaiv, Kropyvnytskyi, Ternopil, Poltava, Ivano-Frankivsk, Chernivtsi, Sumy, Zhytomyr and Dnipro; Odesa announced an evening action. **Only Kyiv's peak rose** (≈2,000 → «близько 3 тисяч», still 1000+); elsewhere the second day was no larger than the first, and no new location appeared, so no dot changes band — the map's second-day change is its framing and the settled statuses, not its geometry. A solidarity action in **Warsaw** is outside the map's Ukraine-only scope, recorded here only as a note. A **third day** was called for the evening of 18 July.

### Third day (18 July), added in the 19 July run

Street actions recurred for a third day in at least Kyiv, Lviv, Kharkiv, Dnipro, Odesa, Ivano-Frankivsk, Mykolaiv, Ternopil, Cherkasy, Khmelnytskyi, Kropyvnytskyi and Uzhhorod. Kyiv ran from ~15:00 to «близько 2 тисяч» by 21:45 (Suspilne reporting Kyiv directly this time), below the day-2 peak. **One band changed: Mykolaiv «понад 150 людей» (was 45 on day 1) crosses into 100–999.** Kharkiv police counted «близько 400» at 20:00, a wave high within the same band. Solidarity rallies appeared **abroad** — Warsaw, Brussels, Sydney — outside the map's Ukraine-only scope, noted not mapped. Rerunning the v3 discovery across the whole wave (gazetteer-grep of every downloaded round-up, batch tier-A queries, targeted city searches) surfaced **no verifiable city beyond the 25**: the four a rival Telegram map flagged (Irpin, Boryspil, Kalush, Drohobych) have no round-up or city-level source, and every gazetteer hit was a false positive — the organiser's surname «Козятинський», the President's name «Володимир», war-attack city lists.

### Fourth day (19 July), added on 19 July

The wave did **not** end: on 19 July street actions ran for a fourth day in ~15 cities and drew the largest crowd of the whole wave in Kyiv — «понад 5000 людей» on Ivan Franko Square (Interfax-Ukraine via LIGA), above the day-2 peak, so Kyiv's figure and the wave peak update to day 4. Kharkiv «понад 400» (майдан Свободи) and Ivano-Frankivsk «приблизно 250» held their bands. **Three cities new to the map: Mukachevo** (LB, UP, 24 Kanal), **Kamianske** (5.ua) and **Uman** (found by the §4.2 gazetteer-grep of the day-4 round-ups — it sat in a 24 Kanal per-city subsection, not the comma-list) — both `unknown` count, both news-sourced (not Telegram). **Odesa surged to «близько тисячі людей»** on Derybasivska (24 Kanal, citing Suspilne), verified in the article body this time (not just a snippet) — a ~7× jump from day 1, so **Odesa crosses into 1000+** (marked contested: single-source, borderline). The round-up also gave day-4 highs: Lutsk «понад 300», Cherkasy «150», Kharkiv «450» (20:30) and Dnipro «понад 150». **Political outcome:** the Verkhovna Rada went into **recess to 18 August without appointing a defence minister** — Klymenko's candidacy was never submitted (Ivan Vyhivsky took the interior ministry) and **Yevhen Khmara remains acting**. The demand is unmet, so the protests continue.
### Checked, no protest found (5)

Drohobych, Bila Tserkva, Irpin, Boryspil, Pereiaslav. **Four of the original six have since protested and are now on the map:** Mukachevo (day 4), then Kamianets-Podilskyi, Izmail and Uman. Izmail is the sharpest correction — Suspilne Odesa states its rallies had run «з 16 липня», so it was protesting on day 1 while listed here as empty. Kalush, listed in the 19 July run as flagged-but-unverifiable, is also now on the map.

**Not marked on the map, deliberately.** These were checked only because they appeared in the prompt's city list or in a rival Telegram map. The remaining ~460 cities of Ukraine were not checked individually, so marking these as checked-and-empty would claim a systematic negative survey that does not exist. Kremenchuk proved the point on day 1: not an oblast capital, a protest **did** take place, and it surfaced only by querying a local outlet directly. Izmail and Kalush proved it again on days 1 and 4 — **a negative here means "not found", never "did not happen".**

### How other cities were searched for

The initial city universe came from the prompt's list plus whatever the national round-ups enumerated. Both are bounded, which makes the search confirmation rather than discovery. To close that, a full gazetteer of **499 Ukrainian city names from Wikidata** was queried through Google News, requiring the name in the headline. The procedure was validated on Kremenchuk, which it finds independently. The **CitySites (0XX.ua)** network was swept in full: 41 domains, found by brute-forcing the telephone-code space, because search engines do not index it (`site:0342.ua` returns nothing). The **Raion.in.ua** network of 44 small towns was swept as well.

**Yield: one location, Kolomyia, 11 people.** Verified separately: Kolomyia appears in **none of the 12 national round-ups**, and in none of the four Ivano-Frankivsk regional sources retrieved here. Its own local outlet covered it, but procedure 2 retrieves local outlets only for cities already surfaced by procedure 1.

**The result is negative, and that is its value.** The sweep cost about 475 queries, hit rate limiting, and changed no conclusion: not the geography, since Ivano-Frankivsk oblast was already on the map, not the scale, not any contested figure. Kremenchuk, also not an oblast capital, **is** listed by KP.UA. National round-ups plus Suspilne regional desks cover everything consequential; outside them live actions of eleven people. CitySites: non-capital sites (Bila Tserkva, Pryluky, Konotop, Nikopol, Irpin, Kramatorsk) carry nothing on day 1; **Kamianske** was empty then but protested on day 4 (5.ua), and Kremenchuk was already on the map via KP.UA. Raion.in.ua: the word Fedorov does not appear at all. No Wikipedia article on these protests exists. No actions abroad were found in the day-1 sweep — they appeared only on day 3 (Warsaw, Brussels, Sydney).

**The tier below 30k inhabitants (364 towns) was not swept.** Google News began returning 503 after roughly 475 queries.

### Unavailable or problematic sources

| Source | Problem |
|---|---|
| **nv.ua** | HTTP 403 on plain retrieval; obtained through a browser, content verified |
| **MykVisti (mykvisti.com)** | domain dead: the origin of Censor.NET's Mykolaiv figure cannot be reached |
| **Konkurent (konkurent.ua)** | JS-rendered, body not extractable: the Lutsk figure is unverified |
| **Ukrainska Pravda, ZN.UA** | full body not served; only targeted extracts obtained |
| **Suspilne / Kyiv** | **no dedicated Kyiv article exists.** For the main protest city, Suspilne offers only the national round-up, stale as of 11:41 |
| **Police / Kyiv City Administration** | no official crowd estimate published |
